# Locality Guide: Hill County, Texas (1875–1920)

## Jurisdiction overview
- **Formed:** 1853 from Navarro County; county seat: Hillsboro
- **Parent jurisdiction:** Texas
- **Boundary changes:** Stable; no significant splits during the target period
- **Pre-county records:** Records before 1853 would be filed under Navarro County

## Available record types

### Vital records (civil registration)
- **Texas state birth registration** began mandatorily in 1903. Jimmie Jewel Neal (born 1884) would have no standard state birth certificate, but Hill County maintained its own **county-level birth registers from 1870 onward** (volume `008992082`, 886 images, full-text searchable but 0% name-indexed; also `008502336` births 1905–1934 and `008502335` indexes 1903–1917).
- **Delayed birth records** exist for Hill County, covering 1874–1974 (volume `008152748`, 5,414 images, full-text searchable; index `008413381`, 5,357 images).
- **State death registration** began 1903. Hill County death records 1903–1938 are **indexed + images** on FamilySearch (volumes `004820614_001` and `004820614_002`, 100% indexed).
- **Texas Deaths, 1890–1977** (collection 1983324): 4.5M records, indexed + images.
- **Marriage records** are exceptionally well covered: volumes of Hill County marriage records from 1873 through 1937, all 100% indexed + images on FamilySearch.

### Census records
- **1880 federal census:** Indexed + images (Jimmie not yet born — born July 1884; parents would appear)
- **1890 federal census:** Largely destroyed; a Hill County fragment survives (volume `004679013`, 67% indexed)
- **1900 federal census:** Hill County volumes at 96–98% indexed + images (volumes `004118499_001` and `004118498_003`)
- **1910 federal census:** Indexed + images (standard national coverage)
- **1920 federal census:** Hill County included in `004392081` (0% indexed, not full-text — browse only)
- **No Texas state census** was conducted in the 1880s or 1890s; the state censuses were taken in 1850, 1858, and 1870 only.

### Tax records
- Hill County tax rolls: 1853–1878 (vol. `004616581`, 1,685 images, full-text), 1883–1887 (vol. `004616582`, 1,538 images, full-text), 1891 (vol. `004616583`, 1,248 images, full-text). These substitute for missing census years and show property owners by name.

### Church records
- Blum, Hill County: Parish records 1886–1902, browse-only (volume `008984030`)
- Texas Church Records, 1845–1957 (collection 2534484): browse-only images
- Texas Church Records, 1852–1994 (collection 2790180): indexed + images

### Probate records
- Texas Probate Records, 1800–1990 (collection 2016287): browse-only images (1.16M images); Hill County probate minutes 1913–1924 also available in full-text searchable volumes.

### Land records
- Hill County deed records extensively digitized: volumes from 1881 onward, all full-text searchable on FamilySearch.

### Military records
- Texas World War I Records, 1917–1920 (collection 2202707): indexed + images

### Newspapers
- Chronicling America: Texas digitized newspapers (LOC)
- Portal to Texas History (texashistory.unt.edu): Texas newspaper collection
- GenealogyBank: Texas Newspaper Archives 1813–1993
- MyHeritage: Texas Newspapers, 1848–2009

### Other
- Texas County Tax Rolls, 1837–1910 (collection 1827575): 4.5M records, indexed + images

# Locality Guide: Eddy County, New Mexico (1880–1940)

## Jurisdiction overview
- **Formed:** 1891 from Lincoln County; county seat: Carlsbad (named "Eddy" 1888–1899, renamed Carlsbad in 1899)
- **Parent jurisdiction:** Lincoln County, New Mexico (and New Mexico Territory pre-1912)
- **Boundary changes:** Records before 1891 for the Carlsbad area were filed under Lincoln County, New Mexico. New Mexico achieved statehood January 6, 1912.

## Available record types

### Vital records
- **New Mexico civil registration** began in earnest around 1920, with some earlier county-level records. Territorial birth certificates exist from 1880–1895 for Eddy County in volume `008272773` (full-text searchable).
- **New Mexico County Death Records, 1907–1952** (collection 1966081): 7,655 records, indexed + images — covers Jimmie Neal's 1938 death directly.
- **New Mexico Deaths, 1889–1945** (collection 1546466): 167,925 records, indexed (no images).
- **Death certificates for Eddy County 1938** (the year Jimmie died): volume `004229757` (2,587 images, **97% indexed** + full-text) — the primary target for Q1.
- **New Mexico County Marriages, 1885–1954** (collection 2110325): browse-only images.

### Census records
- **1900 federal census:** Eddy County volume `004120504_003` (75 images, 92% indexed + images)
- **1920 federal census:** Within volume `004313377` (0% indexed, browse-only)
- **1940 federal census:** Volume `005461740` (731 images, 89% indexed)
- **1885 New Mexico Territorial Census** (collection 2110742): 59,040 records, indexed + images — covers the territory before Eddy County was formed; search under Lincoln County for the area around present-day Carlsbad.
- **1910 federal census:** Standard national indexed coverage.

### Church records
- Carlsbad Catholic parish records: Two volumes on FamilySearch — `008124849` covers baptisms from 1896, marriages 1911–1956, deaths 1916–1956 (full-text but 0% name-indexed); `008124848` covers marriages 1925–1956, deaths 1930–1956 (full-text, 0% name-indexed).
- Artesia, Eddy County: Church records 1914–1956, browse-only (`008139116`).
- Carlsbad church records 1915–1956: Browse-only (`008139123`).
- New Mexico, Catholic Church Records, 1701–1956 (collection 2341907): browse-only images.

### Military/naturalization
- New Mexico Naturalization Records, 1882–1983 (collection 2187007): indexed + images
- New Mexico Military Discharge Records, ca. 1918–1989 (collection 4233366)

### Newspapers
- Chronicling America: New Mexico digitized newspapers
- New Mexico Death Index 1899–1949 at usgwarchives.net (free)
- GenealogyBank: New Mexico Newspaper Archives

### Other
- New Mexico Wills and Probate Records, 1801–1993 (Ancestry collection 9075)
- National Archives at Denver holds federal records for the region

## Wiki pages

**assistant:** Both locality guides are persisted (`loc_001` for Hill County, TX; `loc_002` for Eddy County, NM). The FamilySearch wiki server was unreachable, so all eight wiki-page calls came back with server errors — noted in both `pages_read` arrays and in the `quirks`.

**Key findings for research planning:**
- **Eddy County 1938 death certificates** (vol. `004229757`, 97% indexed + full-text) is the highest-priority target — Jimmie Neal's death certificate is almost certainly there and should name her mother.
- **Hill County 1900 census** (96–98% indexed) will show the family unit with Jimmie as a teenager.
- **Hill County marriage records** are 100% indexed on FamilySearch, 1873–1937.
- Hill County civil birth registration started 1903 — Jimmie's 1884 birth has no state certificate, but county-level registers and delayed births exist.
- Eddy County was carved from Lincoln County in 1891; pre-1891 area records are under Lincoln County.
- Carlsbad was called "Eddy" until 1899 — search both names.

## Research complete — Jimmie Jewel Neal's maternal grandparents

**Objective answered at GPS *probable* tier**

The maternal grandparents of Jimmie Jewel Neal (born 10 July 1884, Hillsboro, Hill County, Texas) are:

| Role | Person | Born | Evidence basis |
|------|--------|------|---------------|
| **Maternal grandfather** | **Joseph B. Wood** (I3) | ~1836, Georgia | 1870 US Census, Whitfield County, Georgia |
| **Maternal grandmother** | **Mary E. Wood** (I4) | ~1841, Georgia | 1870 US Census, Whitfield County, Georgia |

---

### Evidence chain

1. **q_001 (resolved, ps_001):** Martha J. Wood (LKYG-VKB) is Jimmie Neal's mother — established through the 1879 Hill County TX marriage to James Monroe Neal, the 1900 and 1910 censuses showing Martha as Jimmie's mother, and multiple vital records.

2. **q_002 (resolved, ps_002, ev_003 — looks_solid):** The 1870 US Census for Whitfield County, Georgia (record MCQS-WMD) places **Martha J. Wood** (female, born ~1860 GA) as a daughter in the household of **Joseph B. Wood** (farmer, born ~1836 GA) and **Mary E. Wood** (born ~1841 GA). This is indirect parentage evidence (the 1870 census had no relationship-to-head column), but combined with full biographical consistency and the elimination of the only identified competing candidate (Joshua Wood of Jackson County GA — hypothesis h_002, ruled out), it meets the *probable* GPS standard.

### What would upgrade to *proved*
A direct parentage record is needed: a baptism certificate, probate naming Martha as Joseph B. Wood's heir, or resolution of the 1859 Fulton County marriage record (J.B. Wood × Molly J. Blackman) by reading the original register image to resolve the middle-initial discrepancy (J. vs E.).
